[^cite_note-SU-16]: &#x20;The former Soviet republics legalized abortion as part of the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) in 1955.

[^cite_note-cn2-21]: &#x20;In 2021, the Chinese government issued guidelines reducing "non-medically necessary" abortions as a "step toward women's development".<sup>[\[17\]](#cite_note-18)</sup> The guidelines do not provide detail on what a "non-medically necessary" abortion is, nor what specific policies the government has planned to achieve this goal.<sup>[\[18\]](#cite_note-19)</sup><sup>[\[19\]](#cite_note-20)</sup>

[^cite_note-CS-24]: &#x20;The Czech Republic and Slovakia legalized abortion as part of the [Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovakia) in 1957. The law from 1957 legalizing the abortion on request was limited in 1962 when additional approval for each abortion had to be obtained from so called Abort Commission (which rejected about 15% of the requests). The Abort Commissions were abolished by law in 1986. Until 1993 each approved abortion was paid by state.<sup>[\[20\]](#cite_note-renamed_from_1957_on_20230412104558-22)</sup><sup>[\[21\]](#cite_note-potratovost-23)</sup>

[^cite_note-29]: &#x20;In some parts of [Overseas France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_France), abortion on request became legal in 2001.<sup>[\[23\]](#cite_note-26)</sup><sup>[\[24\]](#cite_note-27)</sup><sup>[\[25\]](#cite_note-28)</sup>

[^cite_note-sub-30]: &#x20;Year when all subnational jurisdictions legalized abortion on request.

[^cite_note-YU-31]: &#x20;The former Yugoslav republics legalized abortion as part of [Yugoslavia](34244.md) in 1977.

[^cite_note-33]: &#x20;Including [Svalbard](27441.md). The law legalizing abortion on request was approved in 1978 and came into force in 1979.<sup>[\[26\]](#cite_note-32)</sup>

[^cite_note-36]: &#x20;In the [Caribbean Netherlands](27203221.md), abortion on request became legal in 2011.<sup>[\[27\]](#cite_note-bq1-34)</sup><sup>[\[28\]](#cite_note-bq2-35)</sup>

[^cite_note-38]: &#x20;After explicit legalization struck down by supreme court decision, the law only removes punishment for abortion on request but with no statement about its legality.

[^cite_note-42]: &#x20;The law legalizing abortion on request was approved in 1995 and came into force in 1996.<sup>[\[32\]](#cite_note-al-41)</sup>

[^cite_note-45]: &#x20;The law legalizing abortion on request was approved in 2014 and came into force in 2015.<sup>[\[34\]](#cite_note-44)</sup>

[^cite_note-47]: &#x20;The [law legalizing abortion on request](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_\(Regulation_of_Termination_of_Pregnancy\)_Act_2018) was approved in 2018 and came into force in 2019.<sup>[\[35\]](#cite_note-46)</sup>

[^cite_note-49]: &#x20;The [law legalizing abortion on request](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voluntary_Interruption_of_Pregnancy_Bill_\(Argentina\)) was approved in 2020 and came into force in 2021.<sup>[\[36\]](#cite_note-ar-48)</sup>

[^cite_note-fi-50]: &#x20;Including [Åland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%85land).<sup>[\[165\]](#cite_note-219)</sup>

[^cite_note-af-72]: &#x20;The law of the [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan) consisted primarily of [statutory law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutory_law) and a limited use of [Islamic jurisprudence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiqh).<sup>[\[52\]](#cite_note-66)</sup> The Afghan penal code criminalized abortion and only removed the penalty if the abortion was prescribed by a doctor to save the woman's life,<sup>[\[53\]](#cite_note-67)</sup><sup>[\[54\]](#cite_note-68)</sup> but other sources said that Afghanistan also allowed abortion in case of fetal impairment,<sup>[\[55\]](#cite_note-69)</sup> and rarely for economic reasons if accepted by a religious council.<sup>[\[56\]](#cite_note-70)</sup> After the [2021 Taliban offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive), the new government announced its intention to implement [Islamic law](28840.md) exclusively, and it is unclear which legal grounds for abortion it accepts.<sup>[\[57\]](#cite_note-71)</sup>

[^cite_note-76]: &#x20;The UN source says that this ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).<sup>[\[59\]](#cite_note-74)</sup> However, other sources say that abortion is not legally allowed under any circumstance in Andorra.<sup>[\[60\]](#cite_note-Bernhard_2019-75)</sup>

[^cite_note-79]: &#x20;The UN source incorrectly shows Angola as allowing abortion on request, citing a penal code draft from 2014 that did not become law.<sup>[\[61\]](#cite_note-77)</sup> The version of the penal code enacted in 2020 and entered into force in 2021 allows abortion only in certain circumstances.<sup>[\[62\]](#cite_note-78)</sup>

[^cite_note-82]: &#x20;A 2001 UN source says that abortion must be performed within the first 16 weeks and that it may be permitted after this period under very exceptional circumstances.<sup>[\[64\]](#cite_note-ag-81)</sup>

[^cite_note-84]: &#x20;Before independence, a [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) in the parent country allowed abortion for this ground, but the decision has not been explicitly recognized by Antigua and Barbuda.<sup>[\[64\]](#cite_note-ag-81)</sup><sup>[\[65\]](#cite_note-caribbean-83)</sup><sup>: 14 </sup>

[^cite_note-89]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted in all subdivisions except the [Northern Territory](21638.md).<sup>[\[68\]](#cite_note-nswresearch-87)</sup><sup>[\[69\]](#cite_note-conv-88)</sup>

[^cite_note-93]: &#x20;Also applies to [Jervis Bay Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jervis_Bay_Territory).<sup>[\[72\]](#cite_note-92)</sup>

[^cite_note-nf-98]: &#x20;Most laws of New South Wales and Queensland, including their abortion laws, are set to apply to Norfolk Island after 2026.<sup>[\[74\]](#cite_note-95)</sup><sup>[\[75\]](#cite_note-96)</sup> The Criminal Code of Norfolk Island, which remains in force in the territory, does not prohibit abortion.<sup>[\[76\]](#cite_note-97)</sup>

[^cite_note-aunt-100]: &#x20;Abortion up to 24 weeks may be performed if the medical practitioner considers the abortion is appropriate in all the circumstances, having regard to all relevant medical circumstances, the woman's current and future physical, psychological and social circumstances, and professional standards and guidelines. Later abortion may be performed, if two medical practitioners consider the abortion is appropriate in all the circumstances, having regard to the mentioned matters.<sup>[\[77\]](#cite_note-aunt-99)</sup> These criteria are not considered as allowing abortion on request.<sup>[\[68\]](#cite_note-nswresearch-87)</sup><sup>[\[69\]](#cite_note-conv-88)</sup>

[^cite_note-108]: &#x20;Also applies to [Christmas Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_Island) and the [Cocos (Keeling) Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocos_\(Keeling\)_Islands).<sup>[\[83\]](#cite_note-106)</sup><sup>[\[84\]](#cite_note-107)</sup>

[^cite_note-at-110]: &#x20;If the woman was under age 14 when getting pregnant, no limit is specified.

[^cite_note-therapeutic-113]: &#x20;The penal code says that abortion is permitted for [therapeutic purposes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Therapeutic_abortion) but is unclear whether it means only to save the woman's life or also to preserve her health. The UN source marks it as a permitted ground.

[^cite_note-bh-116]: &#x20;The UN source marks it as a legal ground because the Penal Code explicitly prohibits abortion only if performed without the consent of the woman and of a medical practitioner.<sup>[\[88\]](#cite_note-114)</sup> However, the decree regulating medical practice prohibits abortion unless the pregnancy threatens the woman's life.<sup>[\[89\]](#cite_note-115)</sup>

[^cite_note-bd-117]: &#x20;The UN source does not explicitly mark this legal ground for abortion but says that "[Menstrual regulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_in_Bangladesh#Menstrual_regulation) is available on request for women with a last menstrual period of 10 weeks or less."<sup>[\[50\]](#cite_note-UN2019data-64)</sup>

[^cite_note-122]: &#x20;The law permits abortion for medical reasons without gestational limit, for social reasons up to 22 weeks of gestation, and on request up to 12 weeks of gestation.<sup>[\[91\]](#cite_note-119)</sup> By regulation, fetal impairment is included as a medical reason,<sup>[\[92\]](#cite_note-120)</sup> and rape is included as a social reason.<sup>[\[93\]](#cite_note-121)</sup>

[^cite_note-be-125]: &#x20;Defined as 12 weeks from conception, considered as 14 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[95\]](#cite_note-124)</sup>

[^cite_note-130]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits abortion except to save the woman's life, when the pregnancy is a result of rape or incest, or when the woman is of unsound mental condition.<sup>[\[98\]](#cite_note-bt1-128)</sup> Guidelines for health workers mention grounds of risk to the woman's health and fetal impairment, and define a gestational limit of 180 days.<sup>[\[99\]](#cite_note-bt2-129)</sup>

[^cite_note-bt-131]: &#x20;This ground is only cited in guidelines for health workers, not by law.<sup>[\[98\]](#cite_note-bt1-128)</sup><sup>[\[99\]](#cite_note-bt2-129)</sup>

[^cite_note-133]: &#x20;This ground is established by a regulation implementing a judicial decision, although it is not mentioned in the decision itself or in the law.<sup>[\[100\]](#cite_note-132)</sup>

[^cite_note-ba-136]: &#x20;Continues to apply the abortion law of the [Socialist Republic of Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina).<sup>[\[101\]](#cite_note-134)</sup><sup>[\[102\]](#cite_note-135)</sup>

[^cite_note-br-143]: &#x20;The penal code criminalizes abortion except if done to save the woman's life or if the pregnancy is the result of rape.<sup>[\[105\]](#cite_note-br1-139)</sup> Due to a [decision by the Supreme Federal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ADPF_54), abortion is also permitted in case of [anencephaly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anencephaly), and it may also be authorized by court order in other fatal cases of fetal impairment.<sup>[\[106\]](#cite_note-br2-140)</sup><sup>[\[107\]](#cite_note-141)</sup><sup>[\[108\]](#cite_note-142)</sup>

[^cite_note-148]: &#x20;The penal code says that social demands are taken into account in a conviction for abortion.<sup>[\[112\]](#cite_note-147)</sup> It is unclear if this circumstance reduces the penalty or may remove it.

[^cite_note-154]: &#x20;There is no criminal law in Canada relating to abortion, but provincial medical regulations may limit the availability of abortion services, depending on gestational age and medical reasons.<sup>[\[115\]](#cite_note-151)</sup><sup>[\[116\]](#cite_note-152)</sup><sup>[\[117\]](#cite_note-153)</sup>

[^cite_note-157]: &#x20;The penal code says that abortion may be permitted to an underage woman in a state of grave distress up to 8 weeks.<sup>[\[119\]](#cite_note-156)</sup>

[^cite_note-161]: &#x20;If the woman is under age 14, the gestational limit is 14 weeks.

[^cite_note-162]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted in [mainland China](55079.md) and [Hong Kong](13404.md), but not in [Macau](19068.md).

[^cite_note-163]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted in [mainland China](55079.md), but not in [Hong Kong](13404.md) or [Macau](19068.md).

[^cite_note-co-170]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[127\]](#cite_note-168)</sup><sup>[\[128\]](#cite_note-co-169)</sup><sup>[\[44\]](#cite_note-co2-58)</sup>

[^cite_note-cg-174]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law.<sup>[\[130\]](#cite_note-172)</sup> The Charter of Rights and Freedoms, which is declared to be an integral part of the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Republic_of_the_Congo), says that "Abortion, other than [therapeutic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Therapeutic_abortion), is prohibited and punishable by law."<sup>[\[131\]](#cite_note-173)</sup> It is unclear whether the therapeutic ground means only to save the woman's life or also to preserve her health. The UN source says that only the ground to save the woman's life is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).<sup>[\[48\]](#cite_note-UN2019report-62)</sup>

[^cite_note-176]: &#x20;A [judicial pardon](288593.md) may be granted to the woman for an abortion on this ground.<sup>[\[132\]](#cite_note-175)</sup>

[^cite_note-183]: &#x20;In some cases, the gestational limit is 12 or 24 weeks.

[^cite_note-186]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle), allowed by regulation and [established by treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maputo_Protocol).<sup>[\[139\]](#cite_note-cd-184)</sup><sup>[\[140\]](#cite_note-185)</sup>

[^cite_note-cd-188]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is [established by treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maputo_Protocol), whose application is requested by the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_the_Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo).<sup>[\[139\]](#cite_note-cd-184)</sup><sup>[\[141\]](#cite_note-187)</sup>

[^cite_note-190]: &#x20;If the woman is of young age or immature and so unable to care for the child in a proper way, abortion may be allowed up to fetal viability.<sup>[\[142\]](#cite_note-dk-189)</sup>

[^cite_note-196]: &#x20;Before independence, a [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) in the parent country allowed abortion for this ground, but the decision has not been explicitly recognized by Dominica.<sup>[\[147\]](#cite_note-195)</sup>

[^cite_note-202]: &#x20;The UN source shows East Timor as allowing abortion also in case of risk to the woman's health or fetal impairment, citing the penal code enacted in March 2009 and entered into force in June 2009.<sup>[\[149\]](#cite_note-198)</sup><sup>[\[150\]](#cite_note-tl-199)</sup> However, the penal code was amended in July 2009 to restrict abortion only to save the woman's life.<sup>[\[151\]](#cite_note-200)</sup><sup>[\[150\]](#cite_note-tl-199)</sup><sup>[\[152\]](#cite_note-201)</sup>

[^cite_note-204]: &#x20;This ground is explicitly mentioned in the law only in case of rape of a woman with a mental disability, but it is also established by judicial decision in case of rape of any woman.<sup>[\[153\]](#cite_note-ec-203)</sup>

[^cite_note-er-210]: &#x20;Abortion is permitted if the woman is under age 18.

[^cite_note-ee-212]: &#x20;If the woman is under age 15 or over age 45, the gestational limit is 22 weeks.

[^cite_note-217]: &#x20;Permitted up to 28 weeks of gestation if the woman is unfit to raise the child due to a physical or mental disability or for being under age 18. The penalty for abortion may be mitigated in case of extreme poverty.<sup>[\[161\]](#cite_note-et1-214)</sup><sup>[\[162\]](#cite_note-et2-215)</sup>

[^cite_note-223]: &#x20;Including [Overseas France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_France).<sup>[\[168\]](#cite_note-222)</sup>

[^cite_note-fr-225]: &#x20;Defined as 14 weeks of pregnancy, considered as 16 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[169\]](#cite_note-224)</sup>

[^cite_note-227]: &#x20;The penal code says that abortion may be permitted to an underage woman in a state of grave distress up to 10 weeks.

[^cite_note-de-235]: &#x20;The criminal code specifies that abortion is not deemed an offence if the woman requests it, she has obtained counselling, and it is done by a physician within 12 weeks from conception. (Also, the woman is not punished for an abortion within 22 weeks if the other conditions are fulfilled.) The woman's living conditions are also taken into account in the indication of a serious risk to her health.<sup>[\[176\]](#cite_note-de-233)</sup><sup>[\[177\]](#cite_note-234)</sup>

[^cite_note-gr-237]: &#x20;If the woman is a minor or incapable of resisting, the gestational limit is 19 weeks.

[^cite_note-gw-245]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits abortion only if done without the woman's consent, outside health care facilities or without professional qualification.<sup>[\[30\]](#cite_note-gw-39)</sup><sup>[\[182\]](#cite_note-241)</sup> The law on reproductive health and family planning says that voluntary interruption of pregnancy is authorized according to the law on abortion,<sup>[\[183\]](#cite_note-242)</sup> but no specific law on abortion exists as of 2022.<sup>[\[184\]](#cite_note-243)</sup> A report by the [Ministry of Public Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Health_\(Guinea-Bissau\)) mentioned a gestational limit of 12 weeks.<sup>[\[185\]](#cite_note-244)</sup>

[^cite_note-gy-247]: &#x20;If the woman is [HIV-positive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV-positive_people) or contraception failure, the gestational limit is 16 weeks.

[^cite_note-251]: &#x20;A new penal code, published by presidential decree on 24 June 2020 and amended by the Council of Ministers on 24 June 2025, would allow abortion on request in the first 12 weeks of pregnancy, and in case of risk to the woman's physical or mental health, rape or incest.<sup>[\[187\]](#cite_note-248)</sup><sup>[\[188\]](#cite_note-249)</sup> In July 2026, it was expected to entry into force on 1 January 2027.<sup>[\[189\]](#cite_note-250)</sup>

[^cite_note-principle-252]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).

[^cite_note-hu-255]: &#x20;Up to 18 weeks if the woman is incapacitated or did not recognize the pregnancy due to illness or medical error, or in case of failure of a health institution.<sup>[\[191\]](#cite_note-hu-254)</sup>

[^cite_note-256]: &#x20;Up to 24 weeks in case of prolongation of the diagnostic procedure, or no limit in case of fetal abnormality incompatible with life after birth.<sup>[\[191\]](#cite_note-hu-254)</sup>

[^cite_note-260]: &#x20;No limit in case of "substantial foetal abnormalities".<sup>[\[193\]](#cite_note-in-258)</sup>

[^cite_note-261]: &#x20;Including the failure of contraception.<sup>[\[193\]](#cite_note-in-258)</sup>

[^cite_note-269]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law,<sup>[\[200\]](#cite_note-267)</sup> but it may be included in other legal grounds if the pregnancy causes unbearable hardship, such as significant harm to mental health or risk of suicide.<sup>[\[201\]](#cite_note-268)</sup>

[^cite_note-272]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).<sup>[\[202\]](#cite_note-270)</sup><sup>[\[203\]](#cite_note-271)</sup>

[^cite_note-iq-274]: &#x20;This ground is only cited in instructions to health committees, not by law.<sup>[\[204\]](#cite_note-273)</sup>

[^cite_note-ie-276]: &#x20;If the risk to life or health is immediate, no gestational limit is specified.

[^cite_note-il-278]: &#x20;Abortion is permitted if the woman is under age 18 or over age 40, or if she is not married or the pregnancy is not from marriage.<sup>[\[206\]](#cite_note-277)</sup>

[^cite_note-281]: &#x20;The UN source marks it as a legal ground but it is only [mentioned by treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maputo_Protocol), not implemented in the law.<sup>[\[208\]](#cite_note-ci-280)</sup>

[^cite_note-286]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle) and established by [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne).<sup>[\[209\]](#cite_note-jm1-282)</sup><sup>[\[210\]](#cite_note-jm2-283)</sup><sup>[\[211\]](#cite_note-jm3-284)</sup><sup>[\[212\]](#cite_note-jm4-285)</sup>

[^cite_note-287]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne).<sup>[\[209\]](#cite_note-jm1-282)</sup><sup>[\[210\]](#cite_note-jm2-283)</sup><sup>[\[211\]](#cite_note-jm3-284)</sup><sup>[\[212\]](#cite_note-jm4-285)</sup>

[^cite_note-296]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[219\]](#cite_note-ke-294)</sup> It is also mentioned in the National Guidelines on Management of Sexual Violence.<sup>[\[220\]](#cite_note-295)</sup>

[^cite_note-la-305]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits "unlawful abortion", defined as "abortion not authorized by medical doctor commission". The penal code also lists the [principles of legitimate defense and necessity to save one's life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_of_self-defense), which lead to exemption from penal liability.<sup>[\[226\]](#cite_note-302)</sup> A decision by the [Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Laos\)) states that abortion is medically authorized, up to 28 weeks of gestation, due to certain medical conditions of the woman or fetus, rape, contraception failure, and certain socioeconomic conditions of the woman or her family.<sup>[\[227\]](#cite_note-303)</sup> A [WHO](33583.md) source also shows Laos as allowing abortion on request up to 12 weeks of gestation, citing guidelines for health workers from 2016,<sup>[\[228\]](#cite_note-304)</sup> but they were issued before the penal code of 2017 defined "unlawful abortion" and are not mentioned in the decision by the Ministry of Health of 2021.

[^cite_note-lt-315]: &#x20;If the woman is under age 13 or over age 49, no limit is specified.

[^cite_note-320]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law.<sup>[\[239\]](#cite_note-317)</sup> The UN source says that it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle),<sup>[\[48\]](#cite_note-UN2019report-62)</sup> but other sources say that abortion is not legally allowed under any circumstance in Madagascar.<sup>[\[240\]](#cite_note-318)</sup><sup>[\[241\]](#cite_note-319)</sup>

[^cite_note-327]: &#x20;The law of Maldives is a combination of [statutory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutory_law) and [Islamic law](28840.md).<sup>[\[244\]](#cite_note-323)</sup> The Maldivian penal code criminalizes abortion after 120 days of gestation, except for risk to the woman's life.<sup>[\[245\]](#cite_note-324)</sup> The Maldivian [Islamic jurisprudence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiqh) allows abortion only for risk to the woman's life, without gestational limit, or in cases of rape, incest, or certain medical conditions of a fetus conceived in marriage, up to 120 days of gestation.<sup>[\[246\]](#cite_note-mv1-325)</sup><sup>[\[247\]](#cite_note-mv2-326)</sup>

[^cite_note-328]: &#x20;Only for certain medical conditions of a fetus conceived in marriage.<sup>[\[246\]](#cite_note-mv1-325)</sup><sup>[\[247\]](#cite_note-mv2-326)</sup>

[^cite_note-mr-336]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits abortion without any explicit exception,<sup>[\[251\]](#cite_note-332)</sup> but the UN source says that abortion to save the woman's life is permitted as a general legal principle.<sup>[\[48\]](#cite_note-UN2019report-62)</sup> The law on child protection prohibits abortion except for a "proven medical need",<sup>[\[252\]](#cite_note-333)</sup> and the law on reproductive health prohibits abortion except in case of risk to the woman's life.<sup>[\[253\]](#cite_note-334)</sup> The government has stated that the law of the country permits abortion on therapeutic grounds.<sup>[\[254\]](#cite_note-335)</sup>

[^cite_note-339]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted by law in all subdivisions except [Guanajuato](412827.md) and [Querétaro](222250.md). In these two [states](8196151.md), people who violate it may be prosecuted but not imprisoned, and they may request judicial relief by [amparo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recurso_de_amparo).<sup>[\[45\]](#cite_note-mx1-59)</sup><sup>[\[46\]](#cite_note-mx2-60)</sup><sup>[\[256\]](#cite_note-mx3-338)</sup>

[^cite_note-mx-340]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted by law in some [states](8196151.md) and [Mexico City](18987.md). In other states, people who violate it may be prosecuted but not imprisoned, and they may request judicial relief by [amparo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recurso_de_amparo).<sup>[\[45\]](#cite_note-mx1-59)</sup><sup>[\[46\]](#cite_note-mx2-60)</sup><sup>[\[256\]](#cite_note-mx3-338)</sup>

[^cite_note-mx2-342]: &#x20;The penal codes of some [states](8196151.md) specify a gestational limit for abortion in case of rape. In July 2021, the [Supreme Court](2476388.md) ruled that it was unconstitutional to set a limit for abortion on this ground, but the ruling only granted relief to the plaintiff and did not automatically invalidate the limit in the penal code.<sup>[\[257\]](#cite_note-mx4-341)</sup>

[^cite_note-mxch-350]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[264\]](#cite_note-349)</sup>

[^cite_note-mxco-352]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[45\]](#cite_note-mx1-59)</sup><sup>[\[46\]](#cite_note-mx2-60)</sup>

[^cite_note-mxtl-376]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[288\]](#cite_note-375)</sup>

[^cite_note-381]: &#x20;If the woman is under age 15 or over age 40, the gestational limit is 21 weeks.

[^cite_note-388]: &#x20;In 2016, the government of Morocco proposed allowing abortion in cases of rape, incest, mental disability and fetal impairment. However, the [parliament](3681092.md) did not approve the proposal,<sup>[\[295\]](#cite_note-384)</sup><sup>[\[296\]](#cite_note-385)</sup> and as of 2021 the abortion articles in the penal code remain unchanged.<sup>[\[297\]](#cite_note-386)</sup><sup>[\[298\]](#cite_note-387)</sup>

[^cite_note-390]: &#x20;May be permitted with no gestational limit in case the fetus is not viable.<sup>[\[299\]](#cite_note-389)</sup>

[^cite_note-np-396]: &#x20;Up to 28 weeks if the woman has [HIV](14170.md) or a similar incurable disease.<sup>[\[303\]](#cite_note-np1-394)</sup><sup>[\[304\]](#cite_note-np2-395)</sup>

[^cite_note-397]: &#x20;Including the [Caribbean Netherlands](27203221.md).<sup>[\[27\]](#cite_note-bq1-34)</sup><sup>[\[28\]](#cite_note-bq2-35)</sup>

[^cite_note-ng-401]: &#x20;The Criminal Code of the [predecessor of Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_Nigeria) prohibited abortion except to save the woman's life. A [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) on a similar law in the parent country allowed abortion also to preserve the woman's health, but the [West African Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_African_Court_of_Appeal), despite applying the reasoning of the parent country's decision, affirmed only the ground to save the woman's life in Nigerian law. The Criminal Code and its judicial [precedent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precedent) remain in force in the southern [states of Nigeria](226734.md). In the states corresponding to the former [Northern Region](2292852.md), the Penal Code replaced the Criminal Code and its judicial precedent, and it also prohibits abortion except to save the woman's life.<sup>[\[306\]](#cite_note-399)</sup><sup>[\[307\]](#cite_note-400)</sup>

[^cite_note-kp-409]: &#x20;The criminal law of North Korea, as amended up to 2015, does not mention abortion.<sup>[\[309\]](#cite_note-403)</sup><sup>[\[310\]](#cite_note-404)</sup> In 2015 the North Korean government issued a directive prohibiting medical professionals from performing abortions but did not indicate a penalty for doing so.<sup>[\[311\]](#cite_note-405)</sup> In 2016, the government stated that abortion was "legal" and "provided upon request by the woman concerned for reasons of risks to her life, physical and mental health and fetal malformation", but it is unclear whether these were the only permitted reasons.<sup>[\[312\]](#cite_note-406)</sup> It has also been reported that repatriated pregnant women are [forced to have abortions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_abortion) to prevent children of mixed ethnicity.<sup>[\[313\]](#cite_note-407)</sup><sup>[\[314\]](#cite_note-408)</sup>

[^cite_note-mk-411]: &#x20;May be permitted with no gestational limit in some cases.<sup>[\[315\]](#cite_note-mk-410)</sup>

[^cite_note-413]: &#x20;No limit if it is clear that the fetus will die during pregnancy or shortly after birth.<sup>[\[316\]](#cite_note-no-412)</sup>

[^cite_note-om-416]: &#x20;The penal law prohibits abortion without any explicit exception, but it exempts from penal liability actions done by necessity to protect [oneself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_of_self-defense) or others from a severe and imminent danger, and in the practice of agreed medical activities or urgent medical intervention.<sup>[\[317\]](#cite_note-414)</sup> The law regulating medical practice prohibits abortion except for risk to the woman's life or of unbearable illness, and in case of fetal impairment up to 120 days of gestation.<sup>[\[318\]](#cite_note-415)</sup>

[^cite_note-421]: &#x20;Different sources specify this limit as 120 days or four months of gestation.<sup>[\[321\]](#cite_note-419)</sup><sup>[\[322\]](#cite_note-420)</sup>

[^cite_note-423]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law<sup>[\[323\]](#cite_note-422)</sup> but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).

[^cite_note-ps-429]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion except to save the woman's life.<sup>[\[324\]](#cite_note-424)</sup> However, some sources say that abortion may also be permitted for health reasons and in cases of rape and fetal impairment,<sup>[\[325\]](#cite_note-425)</sup><sup>[\[326\]](#cite_note-426)</sup><sup>[\[327\]](#cite_note-427)</sup> while other sources say that it is not possible to obtain an abortion in any circumstance.<sup>[\[328\]](#cite_note-428)</sup>

[^cite_note-436]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion done "unlawfully" without defining it, and it explicitly permits abortion to preserve the woman's life.<sup>[\[331\]](#cite_note-pg-432)</sup> An opinion of the State Solicitor in 1982, based on court decisions on identical laws in the former parent country, considered that preservation of the woman's health was also a legal ground for abortion.<sup>[\[332\]](#cite_note-433)</sup><sup>[\[333\]](#cite_note-434)</sup> However, in 2018, in the case of a woman who had aborted at four months of pregnancy due to risk to health, the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Papua_New_Guinea) acquitted her because she had been wrongly charged for the crime of killing an unborn child, which only applies shortly before birth (section 312), but ruled that she should have still been charged for the crime of abortion (section 225).<sup>[\[334\]](#cite_note-435)</sup>

[^cite_note-442]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion without explicitly mentioning any exception,<sup>[\[337\]](#cite_note-ph-439)</sup> but in 2014 the [Supreme Court](1335534.md) ruled that [indirect abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indirect_abortion) done to save the woman's life was permitted under the [principle of double effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principle_of_double_effect).<sup>[\[338\]](#cite_note-440)</sup><sup>[\[339\]](#cite_note-441)</sup>

[^cite_note-445]: &#x20;This ground was mentioned in the law but it was invalidated by a [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%932021_women's_strike_protests_in_Poland#Constitutional_Tribunal_abortion_case) in 2020.<sup>[\[341\]](#cite_note-pl2-444)</sup>

[^cite_note-447]: &#x20;This ground was mentioned in the law but it was invalidated by a judicial decision in 1997.<sup>[\[342\]](#cite_note-pl1-446)</sup>

[^cite_note-458]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne).<sup>[\[351\]](#cite_note-456)</sup><sup>[\[352\]](#cite_note-457)</sup><sup>[\[65\]](#cite_note-caribbean-83)</sup><sup>: 14 </sup>

[^cite_note-463]: &#x20;In case of risk to the woman's life after fetal viability, the pregnancy may also be interrupted by attempting a live birth.

[^cite_note-464]: &#x20;Permitted until fetal viability in case of a fetal anomaly that poses a risk to the woman's health.

[^cite_note-sa-468]: &#x20;Abortion may also permitted up to 40 days of gestation for other reasons that are not economic or social concerns.<sup>[\[357\]](#cite_note-sa-465)</sup><sup>[\[358\]](#cite_note-466)</sup><sup>[\[359\]](#cite_note-467)</sup>

[^cite_note-sn-473]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits abortion without any explicit exception,<sup>[\[360\]](#cite_note-469)</sup> but the code of medical ethics permits abortion to save the woman's life.<sup>[\[361\]](#cite_note-470)</sup><sup>[\[48\]](#cite_note-UN2019report-62)</sup><sup>[\[362\]](#cite_note-471)</sup> The government has stated that abortion is authorized in case of risk to the woman's health.<sup>[\[363\]](#cite_note-472)</sup>

[^cite_note-sc-478]: &#x20;In some cases, abortion may be allowed up to fetal viability or 26 weeks of gestation.<sup>[\[366\]](#cite_note-476)</sup><sup>[\[367\]](#cite_note-477)</sup>

[^cite_note-sl-486]: &#x20;Sierra Leone established that the laws in force in England in 1880 would be in force in Sierra Leone from 1965.<sup>[\[368\]](#cite_note-479)</sup> One of these laws prohibited abortion done "unlawfully" without defining it.<sup>[\[369\]](#cite_note-480)</sup> A [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) in England in 1938 clarified that this law always implicitly allowed abortion at least to save the woman's life, and the decision allowed it also to preserve her health.<sup>[\[370\]](#cite_note-bourne-481)</sup> It is unclear whether Sierra Leone applies only the original [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle) or also the judicial decision.<sup>[\[371\]](#cite_note-482)</sup><sup>[\[372\]](#cite_note-483)</sup> In 2015 the [parliament of Sierra Leone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Sierra_Leone) passed a law allowing abortion on request but it was not signed by the [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Sierra_Leone) so it did not [come into force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Come_into_force).<sup>[\[373\]](#cite_note-484)</sup><sup>[\[374\]](#cite_note-485)</sup>

[^cite_note-490]: &#x20;In some cases, the gestational limit is 12 weeks.

[^cite_note-497]: &#x20;Including [Somaliland](51425.md).<sup>[\[382\]](#cite_note-495)</sup><sup>[\[383\]](#cite_note-496)</sup>

[^cite_note-498]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).

[^cite_note-502]: &#x20;The laws of South Korea prohibited abortion except for risk to the woman's health, rape, incest, or certain medical conditions, up to 24 weeks of gestation.<sup>[\[384\]](#cite_note-499)</sup> On 11 April 2019, the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Korea) ruled that the abortion restrictions were unconstitutional, giving the [legislature](601423.md) until the end of 2020 to amend the laws to allow abortion on request with some gestational limit. In October 2020 the government proposed a limit of 14 weeks for abortion on request and 24 weeks for certain other cases, but the legislature did not approve this or any other proposal on the subject before the end of the year, so the abortion laws became automatically invalid on 1 January 2021.<sup>[\[385\]](#cite_note-kr-500)</sup> As of 2025, the legislature had still not approved any of the proposals, leaving abortion decriminalized without a clear gestational limit.<sup>[\[386\]](#cite_note-501)</sup>

[^cite_note-kr-503]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is established by judicial decision.<sup>[\[385\]](#cite_note-kr-500)</sup>

[^cite_note-507]: &#x20;In case of a fatal anomaly, no limit is specified.

[^cite_note-510]: &#x20;From conception.

[^cite_note-512]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law<sup>[\[392\]](#cite_note-511)</sup> but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).

[^cite_note-521]: &#x20;In [mainland Tanzania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_Tanzania), articles 150 to 152 of the penal code prohibit abortion done "unlawfully", and article 230 of the same law permits abortion to preserve the woman's life. Article 219 additionally prohibits "child destruction", meaning abortion after fetal viability, presumed at 28 weeks of pregnancy, but still permits it to preserve the woman's life.<sup>[\[399\]](#cite_note-519)</sup> In [Zanzibar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zanzibar), the penal act has equivalent articles 129 to 131, 213 and 200.<sup>[\[400\]](#cite_note-520)</sup>

[^cite_note-523]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law. A judicial decision by the [East African Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Court_of_Appeal), with jurisdiction over the predecessors of Tanzania, allowed abortion also to preserve the woman's health, and sources state that this decision remains binding after independence.<sup>[\[401\]](#cite_note-522)</sup>

[^cite_note-529]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law<sup>[\[405\]](#cite_note-527)</sup> but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).<sup>[\[406\]](#cite_note-528)</sup>

[^cite_note-tt-535]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law<sup>[\[409\]](#cite_note-532)</sup> but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle) and established by [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne).<sup>[\[410\]](#cite_note-533)</sup><sup>[\[411\]](#cite_note-534)</sup>

[^cite_note-ua-544]: &#x20;If the woman is under age 15 or over age 45, the gestational limit is 22 weeks.<sup>[\[418\]](#cite_note-542)</sup><sup>[\[419\]](#cite_note-543)</sup>

[^cite_note-ae-549]: &#x20;A [Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates) resolution on abortion, issued under the law on medical liability, permits abortion "based on the request of the spouses, after the approval of the committee", "and with the approval of the treating physician for the medical condition justifying the abortion", in the first 120 days of pregnancy.<sup>[\[421\]](#cite_note-ae-546)</sup> These provisions are considered to permit abortion in case of risk to the woman's physical or mental health, and may also include other cases.<sup>[\[422\]](#cite_note-547)</sup><sup>[\[423\]](#cite_note-548)</sup>

[^cite_note-gb-553]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is considered to be included in a ground for preserving physical or mental health.<sup>[\[424\]](#cite_note-gb1-550)</sup><sup>[\[425\]](#cite_note-gb2-551)</sup><sup>[\[426\]](#cite_note-gb3-552)</sup>

[^cite_note-gbni-555]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted only in [Northern Ireland](21265.md), up to 12 weeks of gestation.<sup>[\[427\]](#cite_note-gbni-554)</sup>

[^cite_note-us-558]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is not permitted in some [states](18618239.md).

[^cite_note-561]: &#x20;Permitted in case of a lethal anomaly up to 20 weeks from fertilization, considered as 22 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[431\]](#cite_note-560)</sup><sup>[\[430\]](#cite_note-usal-559)</sup>

[^cite_note-USstatecon-562]: &#x20;This [U.S. state](18618239.md) has explicitly amended its [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_constitution_\(United_States\)) to guarantee the right to an abortion to its residents.

[^cite_note-usaz-566]: &#x20;The law sets a gestational limit of 15 weeks for abortion on this ground,<sup>[\[433\]](#cite_note-usaz1-564)</sup> but the constitution invalidates its enforcement before fetal viability.<sup>[\[434\]](#cite_note-usaz2-565)</sup>

[^cite_note-570]: &#x20;Permitted in the first 23 weeks of gestation if the fetus has a fatal abnormality.<sup>[\[437\]](#cite_note-usfl-569)</sup>

[^cite_note-us4-574]: &#x20;Defined as 20 weeks from fertilization, considered as 22 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[429\]](#cite_note-guttmacher-557)</sup>

[^cite_note-us3-575]: &#x20;Prohibited [after embryonic or fetal cardiac activity is detected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heartbeat_bill), which is possible after approximately **6 weeks** of gestation.

[^cite_note-578]: &#x20;This ground is not mentioned in the state law but it is established by judicial decision based on federal law.<sup>[\[442\]](#cite_note-577)</sup>

[^cite_note-580]: &#x20;Defined as 13 weeks from fertilization, considered as 15 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[441\]](#cite_note-usid-576)</sup><sup>[\[443\]](#cite_note-579)</sup>

[^cite_note-583]: &#x20;Defined as 10 weeks from fertilization, considered as 12 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[444\]](#cite_note-usin-581)</sup>

[^cite_note-584]: &#x20;Permitted in case of a lethal anomaly up to 20 weeks from fertilization, considered as 22 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[444\]](#cite_note-usin-581)</sup>

[^cite_note-588]: &#x20;Permitted in case of a fetal abnormality incompatible with life up to 20 weeks from fertilization, considered as 22 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[447\]](#cite_note-usia-586)</sup>

[^cite_note-593]: &#x20;Permitted in certain cases of fatal anomalies.<sup>[\[451\]](#cite_note-usla-591)</sup><sup>[\[452\]](#cite_note-592)</sup>

[^cite_note-usme-595]: &#x20;Abortion after viability is allowed if a physician judges it "necessary" under the "applicable standard of care".<sup>[\[453\]](#cite_note-usme-594)</sup>

[^cite_note-usmd-599]: &#x20;Criminal law prohibits abortion after fetal viability except as stated in health law,<sup>[\[454\]](#cite_note-usmd1-596)</sup> and health law states that "the State may not interfere" with abortion on this ground before viability.<sup>[\[455\]](#cite_note-usmd2-597)</sup> Sources disagree whether it is actually prohibited after viability.<sup>[\[456\]](#cite_note-598)</sup><sup>[\[429\]](#cite_note-guttmacher-557)</sup>

[^cite_note-usmo-609]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion on this ground,<sup>[\[464\]](#cite_note-usmo1-607)</sup> but the constitution invalidates its enforcement before fetal viability.<sup>[\[465\]](#cite_note-usmo2-608)</sup>

[^cite_note-usny-615]: &#x20;Also allowed after this period if there is an absence of fetal viability.<sup>[\[470\]](#cite_note-614)</sup>

[^cite_note-617]: &#x20;Permitted up to 24 weeks of gestation in case of a life-limiting anomaly.<sup>[\[471\]](#cite_note-usnc-616)</sup>

[^cite_note-usoh-622]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion on this ground [after embryonic or fetal cardiac activity is detected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heartbeat_bill),<sup>[\[474\]](#cite_note-usoh1-620)</sup> which is possible after approximately 6 weeks of gestation, but the constitution invalidates its enforcement before fetal viability.<sup>[\[475\]](#cite_note-usoh2-621)</sup>

[^cite_note-635]: &#x20;In case of a lethal anomaly or a severe brain abnormality, no limit is specified.

[^cite_note-us2-636]: &#x20;A law prohibits abortion on this ground or sets a lower gestational limit but it is suspended by judicial decision.

[^cite_note-639]: &#x20;Defined as 8 weeks from implantation, approximately 11 weeks from the last menstrual period. If the patient is a minor or an incompetent or incapacitated adult, abortion in case of rape is permitted in the first 14 weeks from implantation, approximately 17 weeks from the last menstrual period.<sup>[\[489\]](#cite_note-uswv-638)</sup>

[^cite_note-640]: &#x20;Permitted with no gestational limit if the fetus has a lethal anomaly.<sup>[\[489\]](#cite_note-uswv-638)</sup>

[^cite_note-uy-650]: &#x20;A judge may also remove the penalty for abortion on this ground in the first 3 months from conception.<sup>[\[498\]](#cite_note-649)</sup>

[^cite_note-653]: &#x20;The penal code says that abortion is permitted for "good medical reasons"<sup>[\[500\]](#cite_note-vu-652)</sup> but is unclear whether it means only to save the woman's life or also to preserve her health. The UN source marks it as a permitted ground.

[^cite_note-661]: &#x20;The [law of Vatican City](28336196.md) is primarily based on the [canon law of the Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_law_of_the_Catholic_Church) and applies the [Italian penal code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zanardelli_Code) in force in 1929 with local modifications.<sup>[\[501\]](#cite_note-654)</sup> Both sources of law prohibit abortion without explicitly mentioning any exception.<sup>[\[502\]](#cite_note-655)</sup><sup>[\[503\]](#cite_note-it1889-656)</sup> Article 49 of the penal code lists the [principle of necessity to save one's life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_of_self-defense), which removes punishment for any action that would otherwise be a crime,<sup>[\[504\]](#cite_note-657)</sup><sup>[\[505\]](#cite_note-658)</sup> but the Church's official interpretation of [canon 1398](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_1398) is more restrictive, allowing in such cases only [indirect abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indirect_abortion) under the [principle of double effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principle_of_double_effect).<sup>[\[506\]](#cite_note-659)</sup><sup>[\[507\]](#cite_note-660)</sup>

[^cite_note-669]: &#x20;Depending on the capacity at each level of hospital.<sup>[\[512\]](#cite_note-666)</sup><sup>[\[513\]](#cite_note-667)</sup><sup>[\[514\]](#cite_note-668)</sup>

[^cite_note-676]: &#x20;The law prohibits abortion in case of rape within marriage or under age 18,<sup>[\[519\]](#cite_note-674)</sup> but it is invalidated by judicial decision.<sup>[\[520\]](#cite_note-675)</sup>

[^cite_note-zw-678]: &#x20;The 2014 Guidelines for Comprehensive Abortion Care says "In Zimbabwe termination of pregnancy may be permitted for [HIV-positive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV_positive_people) women if they choose to do so."<sup>[\[521\]](#cite_note-677)</sup>

[^cite_note-gbt-680]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law, but the identical text in the law of the parent country is considered to include this ground in a ground for preserving physical or mental health.<sup>[\[424\]](#cite_note-gb1-550)</sup><sup>[\[425\]](#cite_note-gb2-551)</sup><sup>[\[426\]](#cite_note-gb3-552)</sup>

[^cite_note-awcwsx-684]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is accepted as a general [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle).<sup>[\[65\]](#cite_note-caribbean-83)</sup>

[^cite_note-691]: &#x20;The [parliament](4504372.md) has proposed a law allowing abortion also in case of risk to health, rape and fetal impairment,<sup>[\[530\]](#cite_note-689)</sup> but it has not yet been approved.<sup>[\[531\]](#cite_note-690)</sup>

[^cite_note-693]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law, but it is established by a [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) in the parent country. A UN source states this it in unclear whether this judicial precedent also applies to the Cook Islands, but it lists this ground as permitted there.<sup>[\[532\]](#cite_note-692)</sup>

[^cite_note-cw-696]: &#x20;Although illegal, the government does not prosecute abortions performed under rules similar to other countries, including on request.<sup>[\[534\]](#cite_note-695)</sup>

[^cite_note-gi-701]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law but it is considered to be included in the ground for preserving physical or mental health.<sup>[\[537\]](#cite_note-gi-699)</sup><sup>[\[538\]](#cite_note-700)</sup>

[^cite_note-gu-707]: &#x20;Although the law permits abortions on request, no medical providers in the territory perform them except to save the woman's life.<sup>[\[542\]](#cite_note-705)</sup><sup>[\[543\]](#cite_note-706)</sup>

[^cite_note-gg1-709]: &#x20;In [Alderney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alderney) and [Sark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sark), this ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law.<sup>[\[544\]](#cite_note-gg1-708)</sup> A [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) on an identical law in the parent country clarified that the law always implicitly allowed abortion at least to save the woman's life, and the decision allowed it also to preserve her health.<sup>[\[370\]](#cite_note-bourne-481)</sup> It is unclear whether Alderney and Sark apply only the original [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle) or also the judicial decision.

[^cite_note-gg2-711]: &#x20;Abortion for this ground is permitted in the jurisdiction of [Guernsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey), but not in [Alderney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alderney) or [Sark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sark).<sup>[\[544\]](#cite_note-gg1-708)</sup><sup>[\[545\]](#cite_note-gg2-710)</sup>

[^cite_note-gg3-714]: &#x20;Although not allowed by Alderney law, abortions are provided in Alderney under the same conditions as in Guernsey, as health services in Alderney operate under Guernsey law.<sup>[\[546\]](#cite_note-712)</sup> To resolve the legal contradiction, in 2022 the [States of Alderney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_of_Alderney) passed an abortion law identical to the one in Guernsey, and it awaits a regulation to establish the effective date.<sup>[\[547\]](#cite_note-713)</sup>

[^cite_note-nu-723]: &#x20;A law enacted by New Zealand for Niue in 1966 prohibited abortion done "unlawfully", without defining it,<sup>[\[551\]](#cite_note-718)</sup> but a [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) applicable in New Zealand allowed abortion in case of risk to the woman's life or health, and a UN source states this judicial precedent probably applies to Niue as well.<sup>[\[552\]](#cite_note-719)</sup> In 2007, New Zealand repealed the sections of law that prohibited abortion in Niue,<sup>[\[553\]](#cite_note-720)</sup> but they remain in force in Niue<sup>[\[554\]](#cite_note-721)</sup> as legislation enacted by New Zealand after 1974 does not apply to Niue without its consent.<sup>[\[555\]](#cite_note-722)</sup>

[^cite_note-mp-727]: &#x20;The [territory's constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Northern_Mariana_Islands) prohibits abortion "except as provided by law", and the territory has no law about the subject.<sup>[\[556\]](#cite_note-mp-724)</sup> A law from the [predecessor of the territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trust_Territory_of_the_Pacific_Islands) prohibited abortion done "unlawfully" without defining it, and although predecessor laws remain in force in the territory unless modified, a judicial decision ruled this abortion law invalid for being too vague.<sup>[\[557\]](#cite_note-725)</sup> As a result, although abortion remains prohibited in principle by the constitution, abortion providers cannot be prosecuted for it as there is no law specifying a penalty. Still, in practice, authorized medical providers in the territory perform abortions only to save the woman's life and possibly in case of rape.<sup>[\[558\]](#cite_note-726)</sup> In 1995, an opinion issued by the [territory's attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_the_Northern_Mariana_Islands) concluded that [U.S. judicial decisions allowing abortion on request](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade) also applied to the territory, but these decisions were [overturned in 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobbs_v._Jackson_Women's_Health_Organization).<sup>[\[556\]](#cite_note-mp-724)</sup>

[^cite_note-729]: &#x20;Applies [English law](85441.md) in force in 2010 unless locally modified.<sup>[\[559\]](#cite_note-728)</sup>

[^cite_note-pr-733]: &#x20;The penal code prohibits abortion except in case of risk to the woman's life or health.<sup>[\[560\]](#cite_note-pr-730)</sup> In 1980, a decision by the Supreme Court of Puerto Rico expanded the health criteria to also include mental health, including emotional, psychological, family and age aspects, with no gestational limit. However, the decision still maintained the prohibition on abortion if done without any therapeutic consideration.<sup>[\[561\]](#cite_note-731)</sup><sup>[\[562\]](#cite_note-732)</sup>

[^cite_note-736]: &#x20;Applies [English law](85441.md) in force on 1 January 2006 unless locally modified, in each part of the territory.<sup>[\[563\]](#cite_note-734)</sup> [Tristan da Cunha](31361.md) explicitly applies the abortion law of the [United Kingdom](31717.md) with minor modifications.<sup>[\[564\]](#cite_note-735)</sup>

[^cite_note-sx-738]: &#x20;Although illegal, the government does not prosecute abortions performed under rules similar to other countries, including on request.<sup>[\[65\]](#cite_note-caribbean-83)</sup>

[^cite_note-tk-741]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law. The judicial handbook says that abortion is permitted for medical reasons but is unclear whether it means only to save the woman's life or also to preserve her health.<sup>[\[567\]](#cite_note-740)</sup>

[^cite_note-tc-743]: &#x20;This ground is not explicitly mentioned in the law. A [judicial decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_v_Bourne) on an identical law in the parent country clarified that the law always implicitly allowed abortion at least to save the woman's life, and the decision allowed it also to preserve her health.<sup>[\[370\]](#cite_note-bourne-481)</sup> It is unclear whether the territory applies only the original [legal principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_principle) or also the judicial decision.

[^cite_note-exceptionsList-745]: &#x20;All states and territories, apart from the Northern Mariana Islands, allow abortion to prevent the woman's imminent death (with various definitions of "imminent"), and some for other reasons. Northern Mariana Islands allows exceptions per law, but no law has been passed.\
    • Additional allowance for [risk to the woman's physical health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complications_of_pregnancy): Alabama, Arizona, Florida, Georgia, Indiana, Iowa, Kansas, Kentucky, Louisiana, Missouri, Montana, Nebraska, New Hampshire, North Carolina, North Dakota, Ohio, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, Tennessee, Utah, West Virginia, Wisconsin, and Wyoming.\
    • Allowance for [risk to the woman's general health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prenatal_stress): California, Connecticut, Delaware, Hawaii, Indiana, Illinois, Maine, Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, Minnesota, Nevada, New York, Rhode Island, Virginia, Washington.\
    • Allowance for [pregnancy due to rape or incest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pregnancy_from_rape): Georgia, Idaho, Indiana, Iowa, Mississippi, North Dakota, South Carolina, West Virginia, Utah, and Wyoming.\
    • Allowance for lethal [fetal abnormality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_fetal_abnormalities): Alabama, Delaware, Florida, Georgia, Indiana, Iowa, Louisiana, Maryland, Massachusetts, South Carolina, West Virginia, Wyoming, and Utah.\
    Note that these allowances may have a time limit, which may be as early as cardiac-cell activity (approximately 6 weeks LMP); others may have no limit. Different allowances may have different limits in the same state.

[^cite_note-746]: &#x20;Cardiac-cell activity is generally detectable in the 6th week [LMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gestational_age).\
    Allowance beyond this limit is made, at minimum, for an immediate threat to the woman's life. In general, states that permit limited elective abortion may allow abortion beyond that limit for some or all of the reasons listed above.

[^cite_note-747]: &#x20;Typically, fetal viability begins in the 23rd or 24th week LMP.

[^cite_note-748]: &#x20;The second trimester is variously defined as through 27th or 28th week LMP.

[^cite_note-760]: &#x20;Some examples of gestational age calculated from the first day of the last menstrual cycle:<sup>[\[576\]](#cite_note-755)</sup><sup>[\[577\]](#cite_note-756)</sup><sup>[\[578\]](#cite_note-757)</sup><sup>[\[579\]](#cite_note-758)</sup><sup>[\[580\]](#cite_note-759)</sup>

[^cite_note-761]: &#x20;For example Luxembourg abortion law states: "Avant la fin de la 12e semaine de grossesse ou avant la fin de la 14e semaine d'aménorrhée ...", which translates to "Before the end of the 12th week of pregnancy or before the end of the 14th week of [amenorrhea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amenorrhea)".<sup>[\[238\]](#cite_note-lu-316)</sup>

[^cite_note-769]: &#x20;Also known as the "Menhennitt ruling".

This government publication, reflecting laws up to 2017 (including the Termination of Pregnancy Law Reform Act 2017 in the Northern Territory), lists New South Wales, Queensland, South Australia and the Northern Territory as the subdivisions of Australia not allowing abortion on request. Since then, laws allowing abortion on request have entered into force in Queensland (2018), New South Wales (2019) and South Australia (2022). The abortion law in the Northern Territory was amended in 2021 but still requires approval by a medical professional based on the same criteria as in 2017, thus it is still not considered as allowing abortion on request.

[^cite_note-conv-88]: &#x20;[Abortion is no longer a crime in Australia. "With the exception of the Northern Territory, where abortion remains a medical practitioner's decision regardless of the gestation, and the ACT, where no gestational limits apply, Australian jurisdictions now permit abortion on request up to varying points in a pregnancy."

"On the agreed proposal of the parliamentary commissions, the provision that was read in the bill, according to which it was declared 'not punishable the doctor or surgeon, when he justifies having acted in order to save the woman's life, endangered by the pregnancy or by childbirth', was deleted"; "The vote expressed in agreement in parliament led me to the aforesaid deletion, not to exclude the application of the concept that was expressed there, but because it was superfluous and inappropriate to declare it, providing if needed article 49 number 3, the application of which would be only, and without reason, restricted." (in Italian)

[^cite_note-658]: &#x20;[Criminal abortion in the Italian penal code](https://books.google.com/books?id=ctcVAAAAYAAJ\&pg=PA29), Pasquale Tuozzi, Filippo Serafini Legal Archive, 1902, vol. 10, no. 3, p. 29. "However, if you want to search for a provision in our code that covers the surgeon, in addition to article 45, in which the aforesaid reason is rooted, there is also number 3 of article 49, where it is declared the nonresponsibility of one who acts compelled by the need to save himself or others from a serious and imminent danger to the person, to which he did not voluntarily give cause, and which he could not otherwise avoid. Well, these extremes of the state of necessity all apply in the case of the surgeon, who, put in the harsh condition, not created by him, extinguishes an imperfect and perhaps uncertain existence, to save a certain and real existence, which is that of the woman in childbirth." (in Italian)
